# The harnesses

Generated 2026-10-18 by `ham.citybuildersim.tools.HarnessMap` - every labelled assertion in every harness, under the section it prints. Do not edit; regenerate with `Regenerate maps.bat`.

**114 harness files, 10,605 labelled assertions.** AllChecks runs 114 of them.

## Which harnesses read which class

A class nobody reads is a class nothing checks. Mentions by name, so a harness that reaches a class only through another is not counted.

## AgricultureCheck.java - 43 labelled assertions

> The ground under the loaf.
> 
> WHAT THIS HAS TO PROVE. Not that the city got bigger - it did not, and the
> measurement is in claude/farms.md where a number that will drift belongs.
> What is checkable is whether the tenth sector does the four things it was
> built to do:
> 
>   1. the mills BUY their raw material now, and the chain is four links deep
>   2. crops clear in a band with a real import ceiling, so a city with no
>      fields eats anyway and pays for the privilege
>   3. a farm is ground and almost nothing else - and the clock that runs on
>      that ground is what decides fields against glass
> ...

- **L76 1. the good, and the chain it closes**
  - L79 crops can be imported - a city with no fields still eats
  - L81 ...and exported, so a farming city can ship its surplus
  - L83 ...and stored, because a harvest keeps and that is what a silo is
  - L85 crops clear on the band
  - L86 a tonne costs more to bring in than a farm gets shipping one out
  - L88 crops are sold by the tonne
  - L99 the Industrial Bakery buys crops, which it never did before
  - L101 ...and so does the Bakery
  - L112 a tonne of crops comes out as 52.5% of its own mass, baked, at the big oven
  - L115 ...and at the small one
  - L129 the mills declare crops as an input on the sector, not just the template
  - L131 ...and the fields declare them as an output
- **L167 2. the band, and the city with no fields**
  - L172 nobody growing any - the mills pay what the world charges
  - L175 nobody buying any - the fields get what the ship pays
  - L178 ...and a matched pair splits the difference
- **L183 3. a farm is ground and almost nothing else**
  - L237 a field takes more ground a post than any factory in the game
  - L247 ...and far more ground per dollar of harvest than glass does
  - L250 glass pays for its density in power
  - L253 a field's wage bill is small against what it sells
  - L273 dear ground makes a field cost many times what glass costs
  - L275 ...while the glass is still mostly the glass
- **L277 4. the dial, and what reads it**
  - L280 full relief by default, which is what real jurisdictions do
  - L303 six grain farms is 3,000 tonnes of crop a month
  - L305 the fields sold something
  - L306 the mills bought crops
  - L307 ...from the fields, at home
  - L308 ...and the fields' own crop line shows it
  - L320 the fields are standing on real money
  - L332 full relief takes the ground off the roll and leaves the barns
  - L334 no relief puts all of it back
  - L335 and it touches nobody else's assessment
  - L337 the share on the roll is one for everybody else
  - L340 ...and one less the relief for the fields
  - L343 the dial cannot be set past full relief
  - L345 ...nor below none
  - L356 the bill the fields are actually charged follows the dial
  - L364 the investment desk prices a prospective farm at the relieved rate too
- **L368 and nobody breaks ground while somebody sleeps outside**
  - L371 the sector answers with a reason either way
  - L375 Agriculture is the tenth sector
  - L377 ...and the share register is the sectors and the bank
  - L384 a reloaded city has the same fields
  - L387 ...the same cash
  - L390 ...and the same farmland dial, which is a policy and has to survive

## BankCheck.java - 513 labelled assertions

> The commercial bank, and the families it discharges.
> 
> WHAT IS ACTUALLY BEING ASKED
> 
> Before this went in there were three lenders in the game and none of them was
> anybody: a sector borrowed from BusinessDebtManager, the city sold bonds to a
> market, and a family short of the shop borrowed from nothing at all. Money
> arrived from outside the city and interest disappeared out of it.
> 
> So the questions here are not "does the class compile". They are:
> 
>   1. Does the price of credit actually depend on the bank - since 0.7.7 on
> ...

- **L156 1. the two limits**
  - L178 a well capitalised bank is limited by what the city has banked with it
  - L183 ...and fifty branches on the same savings carry no more: the branches are no part of it
  - L189 a bank with no capital can lend nothing, however many branches
  - L193 ...and capital is what lets it lend, at the regulator's ratio
  - L196 ...so the two limits are genuinely different questions
  - L199 a bank that has lost more than it owns is insolvent
- **L202 1b. WHAT TO PAY SAVERS IS A DECISION**
- **L215 what to pay savers is a decision**
  - L227 a bank whose margin is gone pays its savers nothing
  - L229 ...and says its margin held them under the rate it chose
  - L251 fixture: the rate it chose on all its deposits is more than its margin
  - L254 a bank whose deposits earn it nothing pays its savers their share of what it did earn
  - L256 fixture: the second bank's payroll leaves less than that share
  - L258 ...and never so much that its margin no longer pays its staff and buildings
  - L260 ...so paying its savers never costs it money
  - L271 fixture: the idle capital earned a placement
  - L273 a new bank moves a step toward the share of the dial its funding asks for
  - L275 ...and that rate is what it paid, over the deposits
  - L278 ...with its margin to spare, so nothing held it
  - L296 a bank's first month pays its savers less than it charges
  - L298 ...and no more than the window charges it
- **L301 ...and it does not open counters either**
  - L323 fixture: a branch cost what the closed month paid over the two standing
  - L328 a bank with customers for a third branch, whose fees would cover all three, opens it
  - L331 ...but not without the customers for it, whatever the fees
  - L336 ...nor when a third's share of the fees would not cover a branch
  - L338 ...though the two it has are covered, so neither closes
  - L342 three standing on fees that cover two: the third closes
  - L346 ...and on fees that cover none, every branch but the first closes, at once
  - L348 the first branch never closes on the rule: a bank is its charter
- **L351 2. STRAIN IS NOT A PRICE (0.7.7)**
- **L363 (1) a strained bank quotes what an unstrained one with the same costs does**
  - L389 fixture: one is comfortable and the other is lent out past itself
  - L399 the same prime, household rate and lending rate at every dial from 0 to 20%
  - L420 the city's note is the same off the strained bank as off the comfortable one
  - L422 ...and so is its twenty-year paper
  - L424 ...which is the dial, its floor and its own spreads, and nothing else
- **L427 (2) a new loan's rate is its parts, and is fixed for its term**
  - L452 fixture: part of its money is the window's
  - L453 the funds-transfer price: the dial, the window's penalty on its share, the curve's term premium
  - L455 ...the window's share is what it owes the window over all it borrowed
  - L459 the running costs: a year of payroll and upkeep over the book, or over what its capital carries
  - L468 fixture: this month's write-off runs past the base rate a year
  - L470 the expected loss: the through-the-cycle base, whatever the bank has just written off
  - L472 the owners' return is the one the market prices its shares on
  - L481 the capital charge: the weight, the bank's own target ratio, the owners' return over the ftp
  - L484 ...and a bank with no year on record targets the minimum and the conservation buffer
  - L486 prime is the four added up
  - L489 ...a household's line the same four at its own weight, with no term
  - L492 ...and the capital charge never goes negative, whatever the dial
  - L497 a city with no bank yet: its money is the window's
  - L499 ...and it is quoted the same four parts, not a punitive rate
  - L516 a lightly leveraged business pays prime plus its own expected loss and nothing else
  - L518 ...which at a tenth of its assets is nothing: it pays prime
  - L523 fixture: new borrowing reprices with the dial
  - L525 ...and the loan written before it keeps its rate to the last month of its term
  - L527 ...which is still running
- **L529 3. WHAT IT PAYS, WHAT IT TAKES, WHAT IT KEEPS (0.7.7)**
- **L538 (3) a flush bank passes on less of the dial than one borrowing at the window**
  - L565 fixture: one is flush and the other at the window
  - L568 fixture: neither was held by its margin
  - L570 a bank flush with reserves settles at DEPOSIT_SHARE_FLUSH of the dial
  - L572 ...one borrowing at the window at DEPOSIT_SHARE_AT_WINDOW of it
  - L574 ...so the flush bank passes on less
  - L580 a new dial reaches savers a step a month, not at once
  - L592 a bank paying far over the window comes down to the window's rate, no higher
  - L604 ...and at a dial of nothing it pays nothing, and never less
- **L607 (4) the fees: the households' reach the bank to the cent, a loan's is its share**
  - L640 a month's fee is ACCOUNT_FEE at the expected price level, in today's money
  - L642 the households charged it end the month poorer by exactly the fee on every household
  - L644 ...which is what the balance says they paid
  - L647 ...and what the books were told they would pay, row by row, before they settled
  - L651 ...on its own line of the household books
  - L656 ...and the bank's cash takes it to the cent
  - L657 ...as income on its Fees line
  - L662 a household with no home has no account to be charged for
  - L687 fixture: the households really did borrow
  - L688 a household's loan fee is LOAN_FEE of what it drew, every month
- **L690 (5) the window charges the policy rate plus a quarter of a point**
  - L699 fixture: it really is borrowing at the window
  - L700 the window's rate is the dial plus its penalty
  - L702 ...charged on what the bank owes the window, a month of it
  - L704 ...and the penalty is a quarter of a point: the Bank of Canada's Bank Rate is its target plus 25 basis points
- **L707 (6) the bank's owners are paid on what the tax leaves, and on all of it**
  - L711 the profit the owners are paid a share of is after the tax
  - L713 ...the same tax the city takes
  - L716 ...and a loss is a loss, with no tax to take off it
  - L734 what lands after the close is counted
  - L735 ...while the month's own statement shows it where it happened
  - L740 ...and next month's taxed profit carries it
  - L741 ...once
  - L744 ...and not again
- **L748 4. a real city, and its money**
  - L821 the fixture actually built a bank

## BooksCheck.java - 57 labelled assertions

> Verifies a sector's income statement and balance sheet, off the template.
> Not part of the game.
> 
> REWRITTEN FOR THE SECTOR TEMPLATE (2026-09-11). This used to drive an
> IndustrialHandler by hand - setFoodDemand, setFoodPrice, computeMonthlyReport
> - and read its report lines back. There is no handler now: a sector's month
> is a LEDGER of trades struck into a STATEMENT at the top of the next month
> (see Sector.strike and Sector.bank), and the only way revenue gets onto a
> statement is a Trade in the ledger. So the fixture books the trades a month
> of selling would have produced and checks the statement is their sum, the
> bills' sum, and nothing else.

## BuildMenuCheck.java - 0 labelled assertions

> Verifies that every building in the game can describe itself. Not part of the
> game.
> 
> WHY THIS EXISTS
> 
> The build menu's info card carries no written text at all - every sentence on
> it is derived from the building's own fields, so that no description can ever
> describe a building as it was two rebalances ago. That buys accuracy at the
> cost of a specific new failure: production1 is tonnes of ore in a mine,
> construction points in a depot, kilowatts in a power plant and units of food
> in a mill, and capacity is people in a house, shelf stock in a shop,
> warehouse space in a mill and treatments in a clinic. A card that reads the
> ...

> Proves the central bank's books: that money is made and destroyed on them
> and nowhere else, every price 0.7.0 hangs off the policy rate, and its two
> dials - the holdings (0.7.1) and the advances ceiling (0.7.2). Not part of
> the game.
> 
> WHY THIS EXISTS. Jerus: "the feds sheet would show how much debt it holds,
> like debt to itself aka money printing." Until 0.7.0 the city's central bank
> was the rest of the world - the bank placed its spare cash abroad and funded
> its shortfalls abroad - and the treasury's overdraft was an emergency note
> with no limit, which compounded a broke seed to $193 quadrillion once the
> bank really paid for it. The central bank brings all of it home, and a
> balance sheet that makes money is exactly the kind of thing that must be
> ...

## ChartCheck.java - 103 labelled assertions

> The charts (0.7.23): the player's decisions as the model records them,
> and the arithmetic City History's charts are drawn by - the window, the
> ticks, the scales, the bands, the lanes and the flags, and the years under
> every other chart - held to its own rules without a screen.
> 
> WHY. Jerus asked for charts "just like yahoo finance", crisis labels that
> are clear, and the player's own decisions on the timeline. A decision is a
> flow the state cannot give back, so it is recorded where it is applied
> (DecisionLog) and saved; a chart is a window of months and a set of
> labels, and the arithmetic of both is ChartModel's, which needs no
> toolkit. The screen is checked by eye on the PC; everything it draws by
> is checked here.
> ...

## ExpectationsCheck.java - 74 labelled assertions

> Proves the anchor (Expectations, 0.7.42): credibility won on target and lost to a miss nobody leans against, expected inflation and its floor, the expected price level, the wages' half-and-half indexing, the money constants struck at the level, the currency's anchored drift and its UIP level, and the anchor through a save. Not part of the game.
> 
> WHY THIS EXISTS. Until 0.7.42 nothing in the city read the inflation
> target and the money constants sat at founding money for good, so a push
> ended as a level step and the autopilot ran a city at about 0% a year
> whatever its target (the project's inflation-research.md). The anchor is
> the design that replaced that - spec-inflation.md, sections 2.1-2.5, batch
> P2 - and every rule in it is a recurrence with a constant in it, so each
> is asserted against its own constants, month by month, on a fixture that
> causes the condition.
> 
> What it has to prove, a section each:
> ...

- **L140 1. before the basket is based**

  - L150 ...expected inflation is the target
  - L151 ...and so is smoothed inflation
  - L152 ...credibility waits at KSEED, and nobody leans
  - L153 ...and nothing moves: the month's step in credibility is nothing (0.7.45)
  - L160 fixture: the founding city has played its first two years, its basket not yet based

> looked identical on the output line - and would have quietly cut the wage
> bill, the wage tax, the households' income and the rent they can afford, none
> of which anybody asked for.

> 0.7.1 the commercial bank held every dollar of the city's paper, so a bond

> carries what the households and the central bank hold of it, and the
> households carry their paper as a fourth asset. Two books that must agree

> audit's pools - each of which is a dollar from nowhere if it is not
> ...

> is fixed - so every way it can go wrong is quiet.

## InfrastructureCheck.java - 101 labelled assertions

> The road network, from the curve up to a city that actually jams.
> 
> Three things have to hold and none of them is obvious from the code:
> 
>   1. The response curve is right - flat until it isn't, and floored.
>   2. A NEW city is never congested. The base network exists precisely so
>      that the opening hour is not a wall, which is the mistake the land pass
>      made when the starting allocation could not fit a power plant.
>   3. Building a road actually fixes it, and the fix survives a save.
> 
> The last one is the point of the whole feature. A mechanic the player cannot
> see themselves solve is just a tax.

> A city played for four thousand months, the way a person plays.
> 
> WHY NOT JUST simulateMonths(4000)
> 
> A single long skip is one input. It exercises the month loop and almost

> equilibrium it finds in the first fifty months and then repeats it. Every bug
> that lives in the interaction between DECIDING and SIMULATING - the ones that

> through - is invisible to it.
>

> ...

## MapCheck.java - 240 labelled assertions

> The city map's data and painter: the districts add up to the model's counts every month and nothing placed moves, the painter draws each district's street plan and paints the same pixels from the same inputs, the drawn raster itself is one street network with + junctions apart and every building within reach, the sidecar comes back byte for byte, and a screen costs what the screen holds, never what the city does - at Jerus's size and at five and ten billion people.
> 
> WHY THIS EXISTS (0.7.60, batch J3; the project's spec-land.md 2.5, 2.6 and
> 3). The map is the one part of the city nothing in the model reads, so
> nothing else would notice it drifting: districts that stopped adding up to
> the buildings, a building that jumped across the city when another was
> built, a tile that painted differently twice, a sidecar from another save
> read as this one's, or a screen that slowed as the city grew - the failure
> the design was drawn to avoid; and (J3b) a city of half a million drawn as
> scattered dots on a field, one dot for each apartment block, which is what
> J3's first render of Jerus's city showed. Headless: no JavaFX (the view is
> J4's).
> ...

## MoneyCheck.java - 25 labelled assertions

> Money is conserved: every dollar that leaves a pool arrives in another, or

> The 29th harness, and the one the other twenty-eight were missing. Every

> Does "Start New Game" actually start a new game?
> 
> Plays a city hard, calls newGame(), and compares the result field by field

> that resetGame() cleared the fields somebody had remembered to add to it, and
> the list had fallen twenty-three fields behind - a new city inherited $81,777k
> of construction cash, $15,402k of business debt, 1,868 units of the previous
> ...

> The Policy tab: banded wage tax, per-sector offsets, the VAT, and subsidies.

> Every rate in the game moved from "one number" to "a city rate plus an

## RailCheck.java - 49 labelled assertions

> The railway: what it charges, who pays it, and what it does to the band.
> 
> WHY THIS HARNESS EXISTS. TradeCostCheck proved the decomposition - that every
> delivered price is still the literal it was, and that three quarters of the
> wedge is freight. This one is about the business that now charges for that
> freight, and it has four claims to hold that the earlier one cannot:
>

## SaveFileCheck.java - 509 labelled assertions

> Verifies where saves go and how they are written.
> 
> This is the only part of the game where a bug destroys something the player
> cannot get back. Everything else can be re-simulated; a save written over
> badly is a city that no longer exists. So the tests here are deliberately
> mean: they fill the target with junk, point the writer at a path it cannot
> write to, and check that in every case the file that was already on disk is
> still exactly what it was.
> 
> Path resolution is tested through the pure resolveDirectory() rather than the
> real environment, because a chooser that reads System.getenv can only be
> tested on the machine it is running on - which is precisely the machine where
> ...

## SaveSlotCheck.java - 54 labelled assertions

> Verifies the slot system: ten saves plus an autosave, the version stamp, and
> the labels the menu is drawn from.
> 
> The thing this is really guarding is INDEPENDENCE. A save menu that shows ten
> slots and quietly writes them all to the same file, or draws slot 3's label
> from slot 7's city, is worse than a single save - it invites a player to
> spread a hundred hours across ten slots that were never really there.
> 
> AND THE AUTOSAVE HOLDS A WHOLE MONTH (0.7.52). Until 0.7.51 the twelfth
> month's autosave was written near the top of Game.nextMonth(), after the
> calendar had turned and before the month ran: the file said month N, its
> history ended at N - 1, and a city loaded from it never ran N. Each of the
> ...

## ZoningCheck.java - 72 labelled assertions

> Zoning and the forest law, the model (0.7.107, batch Z1): Zoning, and
> LandManager's ZONES AND THE FOREST LAW, held to the ledger, the treasury,
> the roll and the world's totals.
> 
> WHY. Jerus, 2026-10-08 (the project's spec-zoning.md): "the default is
> free, anything everywhere, but with zoning, you can allocate a % of total
> land or a specific km² for a specific industry, and even provide the
> land at a lower rate, or free" - and a forest law "of not building on the
> forest, or always keep a certain % of forest available". A zone that leaked
> its ground to another sector, a price the city charged and did not book, a
> roll that read the going price for zoned ground, or a cut that reached the
> protected wood would each be a lever that says one thing and does another.
> ...

> and nothing else, so a figure the view worked out wrong is a figure the
> player decides by. A bar whose parts did not add up to the dry ground, a
> held ground that was not what the zone keeps from everyone else, a staged
> zone whose "after" was not what the city reads once it is applied, or a
> build card that refused a zoned sector's building the ground its own zone
> holds would each be a screen that says one thing while the model does
> another. Nothing in the default playtest sets a zone, so this is the
> ...
